NeoWaves THIRD-PARTY NOTICES
Generated from Cargo.lock and assets/licenses/extra.json.

COMPONENTS
==========

NeoWaves distribution terms -
  Kind: distribution
  Licence: NeoWaves-Compliance
  Authors: zukky
  Source: https://github.com/SuzukiDaishi/waves-previewer
  Note: NeoWaves's own source is MIT. MP3 export loads a separate, user-replaceable LAME shared library using the LGPL-2.0 section 6(b) mechanism; LAME is not incorporated into the executable. No AAC codec is shipped: AAC audio is decoded by the operating system's own decoder where there is one, and AAC encoding is unsupported. Open this entry for the redistribution checklist.

Cisco OpenH264 bundled via openh264-sys2
  Kind: bundled-native
  Licence: BSD-2-Clause-OpenH264
  Authors: Cisco Systems, Inc.
  Source: https://github.com/cisco/openh264
  Optional feature: video
  Note: NOT IN THE DEFAULT BUILD. Cisco pays the AVC/H.264 patent pool only for users of Cisco's own prebuilt binaries; compiling the bundled C++ sources, as this crate does, moves that obligation to whoever ships the result. So `video` was taken out of the default feature set. The only binary this project releases is the Windows installer, where open_video_decoder tries Media Foundation first anyway (H.264, HEVC, VP9, hardware accelerated), so nothing was lost. If you build with --features video and then redistribute that binary, the AVC obligation is yours.

Oniguruma statically linked via onig-sys
  Kind: bundled-native
  Licence: BSD-2-Clause-Oniguruma
  Authors: K. Kosako and contributors
  Source: https://github.com/kkos/oniguruma
  Note: Regex engine behind the tokenizers crate's `onig` feature, used by the transcription model's tokenizer. onig-sys compiles the C sources with `cc`, so it is statically linked on every platform and its BSD-2 notice ships inside the executable.

ONNX Runtime statically linked via the ort crate
  Kind: bundled-native
  Licence: MIT-onnxruntime
  Authors: Microsoft Corporation
  Source: https://github.com/microsoft/onnxruntime
  Note: Machine-learning inference for the transcription and music-analysis features. The `ort` crate's download-binaries feature fetches a static library and links it into the executable -- there is no onnxruntime DLL to ship. Static linking does not remove the attribution requirement; MIT still wants the notice below to travel with the binary.

Signalsmith Stretch (C++) bundled via vendor/signalsmith-stretch
  Kind: bundled-native
  Licence: MIT
  Authors: Geraint Luff / Signalsmith Audio Ltd.
  Source: https://github.com/Signalsmith-Audio/signalsmith-stretch
  Note: The pitch-shift / time-stretch engine, compiled from the git submodule under vendor/. The Rust wrapper crate (Colin Marc) is MIT as well and is listed separately with the crates.

SQLite bundled via rusqlite
  Kind: bundled-native
  Licence: SQLite-PublicDomain
  Authors: D. Richard Hipp and the SQLite authors
  Source: https://www.sqlite.org/
  Note: Amalgamation compiled in through the rusqlite `bundled` feature. Public domain; no obligations.

LAME (libmp3lame) 3.100
  Kind: runtime-dll
  Licence: LGPL-2.0
  Authors: The LAME project
  Source: https://lame.sourceforge.io/
  Optional feature: mp3_lame
  Note: MP3 export, behind the `mp3_lame` feature. Built from the unmodified LAME 3.100 source under vendor/lame-3.100 and shipped as libmp3lame.dll beside NeoWaves. The executable imports the DLL and users may replace it with an ABI-compatible build, using LGPL-2.0 section 6(b)'s shared-library mechanism. The LAME acknowledgement, project link, copyright and full LGPL-2.0 text are shipped in THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt and shown in Help > Licenses. The exact corresponding LAME source is in the public NeoWaves source tree. The software licence does not itself grant patent rights; distributors must assess other rights in their jurisdictions.

DirectML execution provider -
  Kind: spec
  Licence: Microsoft-DirectML
  Authors: Microsoft Corporation
  Source: https://www.nuget.org/packages/Microsoft.AI.DirectML/
  Note: NOT REDISTRIBUTED. On Windows the ONNX Runtime DirectML execution provider is asked for first and falls back to CPU silently if it is unavailable (music_onnx.rs). DirectML.dll itself is a Windows component that has shipped with the OS since Windows 10 1903; NeoWaves calls it rather than bundling it, so no redistribution terms attach. Listed here only so the dependency is visible.

Steinberg VST 3 interface -
  Kind: spec
  Licence: Steinberg-VST3
  Authors: Steinberg Media Technologies GmbH
  Source: https://www.steinberg.net/developers/
  Optional feature: plugin_native_vst3
  Note: RESOLVED as of VST 3.8 (2025-10-29): Steinberg relicensed the VST 3 SDK to MIT, replacing the old GPLv3-or-proprietary dual licence. No agreement to sign and no GPL to take on. NeoWaves redistributes no Steinberg source in any case -- the `vst3` crate ships its own MIT/Apache-2.0 bindings. What remains is the trademark: VST is a registered trademark of Steinberg Media Technologies GmbH. NeoWaves does not use ASIO, which moved to GPLv3/proprietary terms.

#ifdef HAVE_NEON

.syntax unified

#ifdef __APPLE__

.text

.macro WELS_ASM_FUNC_BEGIN
.align 2
.arm
.globl _$0
_$0:
.endm

.macro WELS_ASM_FUNC_END
mov pc, lr
.endm
#else

#ifdef __ELF__
.section .note.GNU-stack,"",%progbits // Mark stack as non-executable
#endif
.text
#ifdef __ELF__
.arch armv7-a
.fpu neon
#endif

.macro WELS_ASM_FUNC_BEGIN funcName
.align 2
.arm
.global \funcName
#ifdef __ELF__
.type \funcName, %function
#endif
#ifndef __clang__
.func \funcName
#endif
\funcName:
.endm

.macro WELS_ASM_FUNC_END
mov pc, lr
#ifndef __clang__
.endfunc
#endif
.endm
#endif

#endif

#ifdef HAVE_NEON
#include "arm_arch_common_macro.S"


WELS_ASM_FUNC_BEGIN WelsSetMemZero_neon
    veor q0, q0
    cmp r1, #32
    beq mem_zero_32_neon_start
    blt mem_zero_24_neon_start

mem_zero_loop:
    subs r1, r1, #64
    vst1.64 {q0}, [r0]!
    vst1.64 {q0}, [r0]!
    vst1.64 {q0}, [r0]!
    vst1.64 {q0}, [r0]!
    bne mem_zero_loop
    bx lr

mem_zero_32_neon_start:
    vst1.64 {q0}, [r0]!
    vst1.64 {q0}, [r0]!
    bx lr

mem_zero_24_neon_start:
    vst1.64 {q0}, [r0]!
    vst1.64 {d0}, [r0]!
WELS_ASM_FUNC_END

#endif

#include "SceneChangeDetection.h"
#include "cpu.h"

WELSVP_NAMESPACE_BEGIN

IStrategy* BuildSceneChangeDetection (EMethods eMethod, int32_t iCpuFlag) {
  switch (eMethod) {
  case METHOD_SCENE_CHANGE_DETECTION_VIDEO:
    return new CSceneChangeDetection<CSceneChangeDetectorVideo> (eMethod, iCpuFlag);
    break;
  case METHOD_SCENE_CHANGE_DETECTION_SCREEN:
    return new CSceneChangeDetection<CSceneChangeDetectorScreen> (eMethod, iCpuFlag);
    break;
  default:
    // not support yet
    return NULL;
  }
}

WELSVP_NAMESPACE_END

.endm

  sxtw \arg0, \arg1
.endm

#include <windows.h>

/////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////
// DLL Entry Point

BOOL WINAPI DllEntryPoint (HINSTANCE hInstance, DWORD dwReason, LPVOID lpReserved) {
  if (DLL_PROCESS_ATTACH == dwReason) {
    DisableThreadLibraryCalls (hInstance);
  }
  return TRUE;
}

;*     "AS IS" AND ANY EXPRESS OR IMPLIED WARRANTIES, INCLUDING, BUT NOT

;*     FOR A PARTICULAR PURPOSE ARE DISCLAIMED. IN NO EVENT SHALL THE
;*     COPYRIGHT HOLDER OR CONTRIBUTORS BE LIABLE FOR ANY DIRECT, INDIRECT,

;*     LIABILITY, OR TORT (INCLUDING NEGLIGENCE OR OTHERWISE) ARISING IN
;*     ANY WAY OUT OF THE USE OF THIS SOFTWARE, EVEN IF ADVISED OF THE
;*     POSSIBILITY OF SUCH DAMAGE.
;*
;*
;*  dct.asm
;*
;*  Abstract
;*      WelsDctFourT4_sse2
;*
;*  History
;*      8/4/2009 Created

%include "asm_inc.asm"

SECTION .text

;void WelsBlockZero16x16_sse2(int16_t * block, int32_t stride);
WELS_EXTERN WelsBlockZero16x16_sse2
    %assign  push_num 0
    LOAD_2_PARA
    SIGN_EXTENSION r1, r1d
    shl     r1, 1
    pxor    xmm0, xmm0
%rep 16
    movdqa  [r0], xmm0
    movdqa  [r0+16], xmm0
    add     r0, r1
%endrep
    ret

;void WelsBlockZero8x8_sse2(int16_t * block, int32_t stride);
WELS_EXTERN WelsBlockZero8x8_sse2
    %assign  push_num 0
    LOAD_2_PARA
    SIGN_EXTENSION r1, r1d
    shl     r1, 1
    pxor    xmm0, xmm0
%rep 8
    movdqa  [r0], xmm0
    add     r0, r1
%endrep
    ret

 * \file    WelsLock.h
 *
 * \brief   class wrapping for locks
 *
 * \date    5/09/2012 Created
 *
 *************************************************************************************

#ifndef _WELS_LOCK_H_
#define _WELS_LOCK_H_

#include "macros.h"
#include "typedefs.h"
#include "WelsThreadLib.h"

namespace WelsCommon {

class CWelsLock {
  DISALLOW_COPY_AND_ASSIGN (CWelsLock);
 public:
  CWelsLock() {
    WelsMutexInit (&m_cMutex);
  }

  virtual ~CWelsLock() {
    WelsMutexDestroy (&m_cMutex);
  }

  WELS_THREAD_ERROR_CODE  Lock() {
    return WelsMutexLock (&m_cMutex);
  }

  WELS_THREAD_ERROR_CODE Unlock() {
    return WelsMutexUnlock (&m_cMutex);
  }

 private:
  WELS_MUTEX   m_cMutex;
};

class CWelsAutoLock {
  DISALLOW_COPY_AND_ASSIGN (CWelsAutoLock);
 public:
  CWelsAutoLock (CWelsLock& cLock) : m_cLock (cLock) {
    m_cLock.Lock();
  }

  virtual ~CWelsAutoLock() {
    m_cLock.Unlock();
  }

 private:
  CWelsLock&    m_cLock;
};

}

#endif

#ifndef _WELS_TASK_H_
#define _WELS_TASK_H_

#include "codec_def.h"

namespace WelsCommon {

class IWelsTaskSink {
 public:
  virtual int OnTaskExecuted() = 0;
  virtual int OnTaskCancelled() = 0;
};

class IWelsTask {
 public:
  IWelsTask (IWelsTaskSink* pSink) {
    m_pSink = pSink;
  };
  virtual ~IWelsTask() { }

  virtual int Execute() = 0;

  IWelsTaskSink* GetSink() {
    return m_pSink;
  };

 protected:
  IWelsTaskSink*   m_pSink;
};

}

#endif

 * \file    WelsTaskThread.cpp
 *
 * \brief   functions for TaskThread
 *
 * \date    5/09/2012 Created
 *
 *************************************************************************************
 */
#include "WelsTaskThread.h"

namespace WelsCommon {

CWelsTaskThread::CWelsTaskThread (IWelsTaskThreadSink* pSink) : m_pSink (pSink) {
  WelsThreadSetName ("CWelsTaskThread");

  m_uiID = (uintptr_t) (this);
  m_pTask = NULL;
}


CWelsTaskThread::~CWelsTaskThread() {
}

void CWelsTaskThread::ExecuteTask() {
  CWelsAutoLock cLock (m_cLockTask);
  if (m_pSink) {
    m_pSink->OnTaskStart (this, m_pTask);
  }

  if (m_pTask) {
    m_pTask->Execute();
  }

  if (m_pSink) {
    m_pSink->OnTaskStop (this, m_pTask);
  }

  m_pTask = NULL;
}

WELS_THREAD_ERROR_CODE CWelsTaskThread::SetTask (WelsCommon::IWelsTask* pTask) {
  CWelsAutoLock cLock (m_cLockTask);

  if (!GetRunning()) {
    return WELS_THREAD_ERROR_GENERAL;
  }
  WelsMutexLock(&m_hMutex);
  m_pTask = pTask;
  WelsMutexUnlock(&m_hMutex);
  SignalThread();

  return WELS_THREAD_ERROR_OK;
}


}

 public:
  enum ETaskType {
    WELS_ENC_TASK_ENCODING = 0,
    WELS_ENC_TASK_ENCODE_FIXED_SLICE = WELS_ENC_TASK_ENCODING,
    WELS_ENC_TASK_ENCODE_SLICE_LOADBALANCING = WELS_ENC_TASK_ENCODING,
    WELS_ENC_TASK_ENCODE_SLICE_SIZECONSTRAINED = WELS_ENC_TASK_ENCODING,
    WELS_ENC_TASK_UPDATEMBMAP = 1,
    WELS_ENC_TASK_PREPROCESS = 2,
    WELS_ENC_TASK_ALL = 3,
  };

  CWelsBaseTask (WelsCommon::IWelsTaskSink* pSink): IWelsTask (pSink) {};
  virtual ~CWelsBaseTask();

  virtual uint32_t GetTaskType() const = 0;

 private:

};

#include "arm_arch64_common_macro.S"
//int32_t WelsProcessingSampleSad8x8_AArch64_neon (uint8_t*, int32_t, uint8_t*, int32_t);
WELS_ASM_AARCH64_FUNC_BEGIN WelsProcessingSampleSad8x8_AArch64_neon
    SIGN_EXTENSION x1, w1
    SIGN_EXTENSION x3, w3
    ld1     {v0.8b}, [x0], x1
    ld1     {v1.8b}, [x2], x3
    uabdl   v2.8h, v0.8b, v1.8b
.rept 7
    ld1     {v0.8b}, [x0], x1
    ld1     {v1.8b}, [x2], x3
    uabal   v2.8h, v0.8b, v1.8b
.endr
    saddlv  s2, v2.8h
    fmov    w0, s2
WELS_ASM_AARCH64_FUNC_END

#endif

/// The PUA code points special-cased in the GB18030 encoder.
pub(crate) static GB18030_2022_OVERRIDE_PUA: [u16; 18] = [
    0xE78D, 0xE78E, 0xE78F, 0xE790, 0xE791, 0xE792, 0xE793, 0xE794, 0xE795, 0xE796, 0xE81E, 0xE826,
    0xE82B, 0xE82C, 0xE832, 0xE843, 0xE854, 0xE864,
];

/// The bytes corresponding to the PUA code points special-cased in the GB18030 encoder.
pub(crate) static GB18030_2022_OVERRIDE_BYTES: [[u8; 2]; 18] = [
    [0xA6, 0xD9],
    [0xA6, 0xDA],
    [0xA6, 0xDB],
    [0xA6, 0xDC],
    [0xA6, 0xDD],
    [0xA6, 0xDE],
    [0xA6, 0xDF],
    [0xA6, 0xEC],
    [0xA6, 0xED],
    [0xA6, 0xF3],
    [0xFE, 0x59],
    [0xFE, 0x61],
    [0xFE, 0x66],
    [0xFE, 0x67],
    [0xFE, 0x6D],
    [0xFE, 0x7E],
    [0xFE, 0x90],
    [0xFE, 0xA0],
];

#ifndef OPENSSL_HEADER_EC_ECP_NISTZ384_H
#define OPENSSL_HEADER_EC_ECP_NISTZ384_H

#include "../../limbs/limbs.h"

#define P384_LIMBS (384u / LIMB_BITS)

typedef struct {
  Limb X[P384_LIMBS];
  Limb Y[P384_LIMBS];
  Limb Z[P384_LIMBS];
} P384_POINT;

typedef struct {
  Limb X[P384_LIMBS];
  Limb Y[P384_LIMBS];
} P384_POINT_AFFINE;


#endif // OPENSSL_HEADER_EC_ECP_NISTZ384_H

use crate::digest;

pub mod signing;
pub mod verification;

/// The length of an Ed25519 public key.
pub const ED25519_PUBLIC_KEY_LEN: usize = ELEM_LEN;

pub fn eddsa_digest(signature_r: &[u8], public_key: &[u8], msg: &[u8]) -> digest::Digest {
    let mut ctx = digest::Context::new(&digest::SHA512);
    ctx.update(signature_r);
    ctx.update(public_key);
    ctx.update(msg);
    ctx.finish()
}

use crate::limb::Limb;

#[derive(Clone, Copy)]
#[repr(transparent)]
pub struct N0([Limb; 2]);

impl N0 {
    #[cfg(feature = "alloc")]
    pub(super) const LIMBS_USED: usize = 64 / crate::limb::LIMB_BITS;

    #[inline]
    pub const fn precalculated(n0: u64) -> Self {
        #[cfg(target_pointer_width = "64")]
        {
            Self([n0, 0])
        }

        #[cfg(target_pointer_width = "32")]
        {
            Self([n0 as Limb, (n0 >> crate::limb::LIMB_BITS) as Limb])
        }
    }
}

impl From<untrusted::EndOfInput> for Unspecified {
    fn from(source: untrusted::EndOfInput) -> Self {
        super::erase(source)
    }
}

        super::erase(source)
    }
}

        super::erase(source)
    }
}

Preamble

The licenses for most software are designed to take away your freedom to share and change it. By contrast, the GNU General Public Licenses are intended to guarantee your freedom to share and change free software--to make sure the software is free for all its users.

This license, the Library General Public License, applies to some specially designated Free Software Foundation software, and to any other libraries whose authors decide to use it. You can use it for your libraries, too.

When we speak of free software, we are referring to freedom, not price. Our General Public Licenses are designed to make sure that you have the freedom to distribute copies of free software (and charge for this service if you wish), that you receive source code or can get it if you want it, that you can change the software or use pieces of it in new free programs; and that you know you can do these things.

To protect your rights, we need to make restrictions that forbid anyone to deny you these rights or to ask you to surrender the rights. These restrictions translate to certain responsibilities for you if you distribute copies of the library, or if you modify it.

For example, if you distribute copies of the library, whether gratis or for a fee, you must give the recipients all the rights that we gave you. You must make sure that they, too, receive or can get the source code. If you link a program with the library, you must provide complete object files to the recipients so that they can relink them with the library, after making changes to the library and recompiling it. And you must show them these terms so they know their rights.

Our method of protecting your rights has two steps: (1) copyright the library, and (2) offer you this license which gives you legal permission to copy, distribute and/or modify the library.

Also, for each distributor's protection, we want to make certain that everyone understands that there is no warranty for this free library. If the library is modified by someone else and passed on, we want its recipients to know that what they have is not the original version, so that any problems introduced by others will not reflect on the original authors' reputations.

Finally, any free program is threatened constantly by software patents. We wish to avoid the danger that companies distributing free software will individually obtain patent licenses, thus in effect transforming the program into proprietary software. To prevent this, we have made it clear that any patent must be licensed for everyone's free use or not licensed at all.

Most GNU software, including some libraries, is covered by the ordinary GNU General Public License, which was designed for utility programs. This license, the GNU Library General Public License, applies to certain designated libraries. This license is quite different from the ordinary one; be sure to read it in full, and don't assume that anything in it is the same as in the ordinary license.

The reason we have a separate public license for some libraries is that they blur the distinction we usually make between modifying or adding to a program and simply using it. Linking a program with a library, without changing the library, is in some sense simply using the library, and is analogous to running a utility program or application program. However, in a textual and legal sense, the linked executable is a combined work, a derivative of the original library, and the ordinary General Public License treats it as such.

Because of this blurred distinction, using the ordinary General Public License for libraries did not effectively promote software sharing, because most developers did not use the libraries. We concluded that weaker conditions might promote sharing better.

However, unrestricted linking of non-free programs would deprive the users of those programs of all benefit from the free status of the libraries themselves. This Library General Public License is intended to permit developers of non-free programs to use free libraries, while preserving your freedom as a user of such programs to change the free libraries that are incorporated in them. (We have not seen how to achieve this as regards changes in header files, but we have achieved it as regards changes in the actual functions of the Library.) The hope is that this will lead to faster development of free libraries.

The precise terms and conditions for copying, distribution and modification follow. Pay close attention to the difference between a "work based on the library" and a "work that uses the library". The former contains code derived from the library, while the latter only works together with the library.

Note that it is possible for a library to be covered by the ordinary General Public License rather than by this special one.

/*

clang fuzzing harness for qoi_decode

Compile and run with:
	clang -fsanitize=address,fuzzer -g -O0 qoifuzz.c && ./a.out

Dominic Szablewski - https://phoboslab.org


-- LICENSE: The MIT License(MIT)

Copyright(c) 2021 Dominic Szablewski

Permission is hereby granted, free of charge, to any person obtaining a copy of
this software and associated documentation files(the "Software"), to deal in
the Software without restriction, including without limitation the rights to
use, copy, modify, merge, publish, distribute, sublicense, and / or sell copies
of the Software, and to permit persons to whom the Software is furnished to do
so, subject to the following conditions :
The above copyright notice and this permission notice shall be included in all
copies or substantial portions of the Software.
THE SOFTWARE IS PROVIDED "AS IS", WITHOUT WARRANTY OF ANY KIND, EXPRESS OR
IMPLIED, INCLUDING BUT NOT LIMITED TO THE WARRANTIES OF MERCHANTABILITY,
FITNESS FOR A PARTICULAR PURPOSE AND NONINFRINGEMENT.IN NO EVENT SHALL THE
AUTHORS OR COPYRIGHT HOLDERS BE LIABLE FOR ANY CLAIM, DAMAGES OR OTHER
LIABILITY, WHETHER IN AN ACTION OF CONTRACT, TORT OR OTHERWISE, ARISING FROM,
OUT OF OR IN CONNECTION WITH THE SOFTWARE OR THE USE OR OTHER DEALINGS IN THE
SOFTWARE.

*/


#define QOI_IMPLEMENTATION
#include "qoi.h"
#include <stddef.h>
#include <stdint.h>

int LLVMFuzzerTestOneInput(const uint8_t *data, size_t size) {
	int w, h;
	if (size < 4) {
		return 0;
	}

	qoi_desc desc;
	void* decoded = qoi_decode((void*)(data + 4), (int)(size - 4), &desc, *((int *)data));
	if (decoded != NULL) {
		free(decoded);
	}
	return 0;
}

1. Definitions
--------------

1.1. "Contributor"
    means each individual or legal entity that creates, contributes to
    the creation of, or owns Covered Software.

1.2. "Contributor Version"
    means the combination of the Contributions of others (if any) used
    by a Contributor and that particular Contributor's Contribution.

1.3. "Contribution"
    means Covered Software of a particular Contributor.

1.4. "Covered Software"
    means Source Code Form to which the initial Contributor has attached
    the notice in Exhibit A, the Executable Form of such Source Code
    Form, and Modifications of such Source Code Form, in each case
    including portions thereof.

1.5. "Incompatible With Secondary Licenses"
    means

    (a) that the initial Contributor has attached the notice described
        in Exhibit B to the Covered Software; or

    (b) that the Covered Software was made available under the terms of
        version 1.1 or earlier of the License, but not also under the
        terms of a Secondary License.

1.6. "Executable Form"
    means any form of the work other than Source Code Form.

1.7. "Larger Work"
    means a work that combines Covered Software with other material, in
    a separate file or files, that is not Covered Software.

1.8. "License"
    means this document.

1.9. "Licensable"
    means having the right to grant, to the maximum extent possible,
    whether at the time of the initial grant or subsequently, any and
    all of the rights conveyed by this License.

1.10. "Modifications"
    means any of the following:

    (a) any file in Source Code Form that results from an addition to,
        deletion from, or modification of the contents of Covered
        Software; or

    (b) any new file in Source Code Form that contains any Covered
        Software.

1.11. "Patent Claims" of a Contributor
    means any patent claim(s), including without limitation, method,
    process, and apparatus claims, in any patent Licensable by such
    Contributor that would be infringed, but for the grant of the
    License, by the making, using, selling, offering for sale, having
    made, import, or transfer of either its Contributions or its
    Contributor Version.

1.12. "Secondary License"
    means either the GNU General Public License, Version 2.0, the GNU
    Lesser General Public License, Version 2.1, the GNU Affero General
    Public License, Version 3.0, or any later versions of those
    licenses.

1.13. "Source Code Form"
    means the form of the work preferred for making modifications.

1.14. "You" (or "Your")
    means an individual or a legal entity exercising rights under this
    License. For legal entities, "You" includes any entity that
    controls, is controlled by, or is under common control with You. For
    purposes of this definition, "control" means (a) the power, direct
    or indirect, to cause the direction or management of such entity,
    whether by contract or otherwise, or (b) ownership of more than
    fifty percent (50%) of the outstanding shares or beneficial
    ownership of such entity.

3. Responsibilities

Modifications that You create or to which You contribute, must be under

Code Form of the Covered Software is governed by the terms of this
License, and how they can obtain a copy of this License. You may not
attempt to alter or restrict the recipients' rights in the Source Code
Form.

3.2. Distribution of Executable Form

(a) such Covered Software must also be made available in Source Code
    Form, as described in Section 3.1, and You must inform recipients of
    the Executable Form how they can obtain a copy of such Source Code
    Form by reasonable means in a timely manner, at a charge no more
    than the cost of distribution to the recipient; and

(b) You may distribute such Executable Form under the terms of this
    License, or sublicense it under different terms, provided that the
    license for the Executable Form does not attempt to limit or alter
    the recipients' rights in the Source Code Form under this License.

3.3. Distribution of a Larger Work

You may create and distribute a Larger Work under terms of Your choice,
provided that You also comply with the requirements of this License for
the Covered Software. If the Larger Work is a combination of Covered
Software with a work governed by one or more Secondary Licenses, and the
Covered Software is not Incompatible With Secondary Licenses, this
License permits You to additionally distribute such Covered Software
under the terms of such Secondary License(s), so that the recipient of
the Larger Work may, at their option, further distribute the Covered
Software under the terms of either this License or such Secondary
License(s).

3.4. Notices

You may not remove or alter the substance of any license notices
(including copyright notices, patent notices, disclaimers of warranty,
or limitations of liability) contained within the Source Code Form of
the Covered Software, except that You may alter any license notices to
the extent required to remedy known factual inaccuracies.

3.5. Application of Additional Terms

You may choose to offer, and to charge a fee for, warranty, support,
indemnity or liability obligations to one or more recipients of Covered
Software. However, You may do so only on Your own behalf, and not on
behalf of any Contributor. You must make it absolutely clear that any
such warranty, support, indemnity, or liability obligation is offered by
You alone, and You hereby agree to indemnify every Contributor for any
liability incurred by such Contributor as a result of warranty, support,
indemnity or liability terms You offer. You may include additional
disclaimers of warranty and limitations of liability specific to any
jurisdiction.

4. Inability to Comply Due to Statute or Regulation
---------------------------------------------------

If it is impossible for You to comply with any of the terms of this
License with respect to some or all of the Covered Software due to
statute, judicial order, or regulation then You must: (a) comply with
the terms of this License to the maximum extent possible; and (b)
describe the limitations and the code they affect. Such description must
be placed in a text file included with all distributions of the Covered
Software under this License. Except to the extent prohibited by statute
or regulation, such description must be sufficiently detailed for a
recipient of ordinary skill to be able to understand it.

Microsoft DirectML (called, not redistributed) (Microsoft-DirectML)
------------------------------------------------------------------------
Microsoft DirectML — called, not redistributed

Copyright (c) Microsoft Corporation.

On Windows, NeoWaves asks ONNX Runtime for the DirectML execution provider so
that transcription and music analysis can run on the GPU, and falls back to the
CPU provider silently when it is not available (see `music_onnx.rs`, where the
provider is built with `.fail_silently()`).

NeoWaves does not ship DirectML. `DirectML.dll` is a Windows component that has
been part of the operating system since Windows 10 version 1903, and the
installer does not copy it. ONNX Runtime itself is statically linked into the
executable through the `ort` crate, so there is no `onnxruntime.dll` or
provider DLL alongside it either.

Because nothing is redistributed, the Microsoft.AI.DirectML redistribution
terms do not attach to a NeoWaves build. This entry exists so the dependency is
visible rather than because an obligation follows from it.

If a future build ever bundles a specific DirectML redistributable instead of
using the OS copy, that changes: the Microsoft Software License Terms shipped
with the Microsoft.AI.DirectML package would then apply, permitting
redistribution as part of an application while prohibiting reverse engineering
and standalone distribution.

https://learn.microsoft.com/windows/ai/directml/dml
https://www.nuget.org/packages/Microsoft.AI.DirectML/License

What you may do with a NeoWaves build (NeoWaves-Compliance)
------------------------------------------------------------------------
What you may do with a NeoWaves build

NeoWaves's own source code is MIT licensed and may be used commercially.
Third-party components retain their own terms; this notice records how the
released Windows build satisfies the terms that require action.

THE SHORT VERSION
-----------------

  * NeoWaves's own code is MIT. See LICENSE.
  * MP3 export uses LAME 3.100 as a separate, user-replaceable shared library.
    LAME is covered by the LGPL; it is not incorporated into neowaves.exe.
  * No AAC codec is shipped. No Fraunhofer FDK AAC code and no Symphonia AAC
    decoder are in the dependency graph. AAC audio is decoded only by the
    decoder the operating system already provides, and AAC encoding is not
    supported at all.
  * MPL-2.0 dependencies such as Symphonia and option-ext are commercially
    usable file-level copyleft. If their source files are modified, those
    modified files must remain available under MPL-2.0.
  * No strong-copyleft GPL/AGPL application dependency is shipped.

HOW LGPL-2.0 SECTION 6 IS SATISFIED
-----------------------------------

MP3 export calls libmp3lame through NeoWaves's own small MIT-licensed FFI.
`build.rs` compiles the unmodified LAME 3.100 source under
`vendor/lame-3.100` into libmp3lame.dll, and the installer places that DLL next
to neowaves.exe.

  Section 6(b) permits a suitable shared-library mechanism that uses a copy of
  the Library already present on the system and operates properly with a
  compatible modified version. neowaves.exe imports libmp3lame.DLL, which
  remains a separate file in the installation directory. No signature or
  version lock stops an ABI-compatible replacement.

The LAME use acknowledgement, project link, copyright and full LGPL-2.0 text
are in README.md, THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt and Help > Licenses.

NeoWaves is MIT licensed and therefore imposes no restriction on reverse
engineering changes needed to debug such a replacement. The exact
corresponding LAME source shipped in the DLL is committed under
`vendor/lame-3.100` in the public NeoWaves source repository. Keep that source
available for every distributed binary for at least the period required by
the LGPL offer you make to recipients.

COMMERCIAL OR CLOSED-SOURCE DISTRIBUTION
----------------------------------------

The LGPL does not require an application using a replaceable shared library to
publish the application's own source. A commercial distributor must still:

  * ship libmp3lame.dll as a replaceable file rather than folding it into the
    executable;
  * preserve the LAME/LGPL notices and full licence texts;
  * provide the exact corresponding LAME source (including any modifications);
  * not prohibit reverse engineering for debugging modifications to LAME.

This repository already supplies the technical pieces, but a distributor is
responsible for its own product terms and source-offer retention. This is a
technical compliance record, not legal advice. The software licence does not
itself grant patent or other third-party rights; distributors must assess
those rights in the jurisdictions where they distribute the product.

INSTALLER BUILD TOOL
--------------------

NSIS (Nullsoft Scriptable Install System) builds the Windows installer. It is a
build-time tool: nothing from NSIS is installed beside NeoWaves. The only NSIS
output that reaches anyone is the setup executable itself, which the user runs
once.

NSIS is licensed under the zlib/libpng licence, which grants permission to use
the software for any purpose, including commercial applications. No fee is
charged, none is requested, and an acknowledgement is welcomed but not
required.

The LZMA compression module NSIS uses is under the Common Public License
version 1.0, and its authors grant an explicit exception permitting code to be
linked or bound to that module without becoming subject to the CPL. Only
modifications to the module itself carry CPL terms. NeoWaves modifies no NSIS
module, so the generated installer takes on no obligation from it.

Nothing about the installer build tool has to be bought, negotiated or waived
before a commercial release of NeoWaves, and no build produced by this
repository is restricted to testing for that reason. The current terms:

    https://nsis.sourceforge.io/License

REDISTRIBUTED AND OPTIONAL NATIVE CODE
--------------------------------------

libmp3lame.dll is the codec DLL copied by the Windows installer. ONNX Runtime,
Oniguruma and SQLite remain linked into the executable and their notices are
also reproduced. DirectML is called from Windows and is not redistributed.

Cisco OpenH264 is behind the `video` feature and is off by default. The Windows
release uses Media Foundation for video pictures instead. Anyone enabling
`video` and redistributing that build must review Cisco's binary/patent terms
for their own distribution.

CODECS BORROWED FROM THE OPERATING SYSTEM
-----------------------------------------

Two formats are decoded by code that is part of Windows rather than part of
this product: AAC audio, and video pictures (H.264, HEVC and whatever else the
machine has). Both are reached through the documented Media Foundation API.
Nothing is redistributed, statically linked or bundled for either one, and the
installer copies no codec for them.

Where an operating system offers no such decoder, the format is reported as
unsupported instead of falling back to a bundled codec: an AAC video still
shows its picture on a silent timeline. That is the deliberate shape of the
arrangement, and it is why removing a decoder is a supported outcome rather
than a defect.

As stated above, a software licence does not itself grant patent rights. This
records only that no codec for these formats is distributed with NeoWaves.

BUILD WITHOUT MP3 EXPORT
------------------------

    cargo build --release --no-default-features \
        --features glow,plugin_native_vst3,plugin_native_clap

This removes LAME and MP3 export. MP3 decoding remains available through
Symphonia. No build carries an AAC codec; AAC audio decodes only through the
operating system's own decoder, as described above. This is an LGPL-free build,
not a claim that every dependency is permissive: MPL-2.0 components remain.

RELEASE CHECKLIST
-----------------

For every release, verify that libmp3lame.dll is beside neowaves.exe, the EXE's
import table names libmp3lame.DLL, LICENSE and THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt are in
the installer, and the committed licence snapshot matches Cargo.lock.

SQLite public domain dedication (SQLite-PublicDomain)
------------------------------------------------------------------------
SQLite Is Public Domain

All of the code and documentation in SQLite has been dedicated to the public
domain by the authors. All code authors, and representatives of the companies
they work for, have signed affidavits dedicating their contributions to the
public domain and originals of those signed affidavits are stored in a firesafe
at the main offices of Hwaci. Anyone is free to copy, modify, publish, use,
compile, sell, or distribute the original SQLite code, either in source code
form or as a compiled binary, for any purpose, commercial or non-commercial,
and by any means.

The previous paragraph applies to the deliverable code and documentation in
SQLite - those parts of the SQLite library that you actually bundle and ship
with a larger application. Some scripts used as part of the build process (for
example the "configure" scripts generated by autoconf) might fall under other
open-source licenses. Nothing from these build scripts ever reaches the final
deliverable SQLite library, however, and so the licenses associated with those
scripts should not be a factor in assessing your rights to copy and use the
SQLite library.

All of the deliverable code in SQLite has been written from scratch. No code
has been taken from other projects or from the open internet. Every line of
code can be traced back to its original author, and all of those authors have
public domain dedications on file. So the SQLite code base is clean and is
uncontaminated with licensed code from other projects.

Source: https://www.sqlite.org/copyright.html

Steinberg VST 3 SDK / VST trademark (Steinberg-VST3)
------------------------------------------------------------------------
Steinberg VST 3 — SDK licence and trademark

LICENCE
-------

Since VST 3.8 (announced 2025-10-29) the VST 3 SDK is released under the MIT
License:

    MIT License
    Copyright (c) 2026, Steinberg Media Technologies GmbH

    Permission is hereby granted, free of charge, to any person obtaining a
    copy of this software and associated documentation files (the "Software"),
    to deal in the Software without restriction, including without limitation
    the rights to use, copy, modify, merge, publish, distribute, sublicense,
    and/or sell copies of the Software, and to permit persons to whom the
    Software is furnished to do so, subject to the following conditions:

    The above copyright notice and this permission notice shall be included in
    all copies or substantial portions of the Software.

    THE SOFTWARE IS PROVIDED "AS IS", WITHOUT ANY WARRANTY OF ANY KIND,
    EXPRESS OR IMPLIED, INCLUDING BUT NOT LIMITED TO THE WARRANTIES OF
    MERCHANTABILITY, FITNESS FOR A PARTICULAR PURPOSE AND NONINFRINGEMENT. IN
    NO EVENT SHALL THE AUTHORS OR COPYRIGHT HOLDERS BE LIABLE FOR ANY CLAIM,
    DAMAGES OR OTHER LIABILITY, WHETHER IN AN ACTION OF CONTRACT, TORT OR
    OTHERWISE, ARISING FROM, OUT OF OR IN CONNECTION WITH THE SOFTWARE OR THE
    USE OR OTHER DEALINGS IN THE SOFTWARE.

Before that release the SDK was dual-licensed GPLv3 / proprietary, and a
commercial VST 3 host had to either accept GPLv3 for its binaries or sign
Steinberg's own agreement. Neither applies any more.

NeoWaves in any case redistributes no Steinberg source. It reaches VST 3
through the `vst3` Rust crate, which supplies its own MIT OR Apache-2.0
bindings to the VST 3 C interface and ships no SDK files.

TRADEMARK
---------

The licence change covers copyright only. "VST" remains a registered trademark
of Steinberg Media Technologies GmbH.

    VST is a registered trademark of Steinberg Media Technologies GmbH.

Under the MIT licence, *using the trademark at all is optional*: nothing
obliges a project to display the VST name or the VST Compatible logo, and
declining to use them carries no obligation whatsoever. Steinberg's usage
guidelines attach only if you choose to brand with it, and then they ask for
the ® symbol on first use, the attribution line above in product credits and
documentation, and the VST Compatible logo on packaging and on every web page
promoting the product. Not following them is a trademark matter and does not
affect the MIT rights to the SDK.

NeoWaves does not brand itself with VST. Where "VST3" appears it names a plug-in
format, a file extension or the operating system's own plug-in folder -- the
descriptive use every DAW makes of it -- in three user-visible strings: the
plug-in file-picker filter and two "not a recognized VST3/CLAP plugin" errors.
The attribution above is reproduced here regardless, because it costs nothing
and is the courteous thing to do.

If the product name, icon or marketing ever adopts the VST name or logo, the
guidelines above start to apply and should be read in full:
https://steinbergmedia.github.io/vst3_dev_portal/pages/VST+3+Licensing/Usage+guidelines.html

A NOTE ON ASIO
--------------

The same round of changes moved ASIO in the opposite direction — to GPLv3 /
proprietary. NeoWaves does not use ASIO: `cpal` is built without its `asio`
feature and no ASIO crate appears in Cargo.lock. Enabling it would introduce a
strong-copyleft or separately licensed interface and requires a fresh review.

See https://www.steinberg.net/press/2025/vst-3-8/ and
https://github.com/steinbergmedia/vst3sdk

Universal Category System terms of use (UCS-Terms)
------------------------------------------------------------------------
Universal Category System (UCS)

The Universal Category System is a public, community-driven category list for
sound effects libraries, published at https://universalcategorysystem.com/ .

The UCS initiative publishes the category list free of charge for everyone to
use, in both commercial and non-commercial contexts, and asks only that the
system be used as published rather than forked into incompatible variants.

NeoWaves embeds the UCS v8.2.1 category list (CatID / Category /
SubCategory / Explanations / Synonyms) as offline lookup data in
`src/metadata/data/ucs_8_2_1.tsv`. No UCS audio content is redistributed.

Source workbook: "UCS v8.2.1 Full List.xlsx"
